   <figure class='figure'>
<!-- l. 1069 --><p class='noindent' id='outofdistribution-evaluation-on-ifeval-test-asr-of-the-two-final-models-ft-agg-against-all-six-routers-on-heldout-instructionfollowing-prompts-that-are-new-to-both-the-paraphraser-and-the-routers-the-legend-reports-each-models-mean-semantic-similarity-the-cosine-similarity-between-the-stellaenbv-embeddings-of-the-original-query-q-and-its-paraphrase-q-adv-averaged-over-the-prompts-sectionref-subsecevaluation-the-attack-generalizes-offdistribution-on-the-training-routers-chayan-for-ftagg-routellmbert-routellmcausal-reproducing-the-indistribution-ranking-at-comparable-similarity-while-the-heldout-routers-stay-essentially-unattacked-mf-sw-at-r-generalization-is-therefore-to-new-data-not-new-routers'><img alt='PIC' src='report/figures/RESULTS/OOD-IFEVAL.png' /> <a id='x1-43007r10'></a>
</p>
<figcaption class='caption'><span class='id'>Figure 10:        </span><span class='content'>Out-of-distribution        evaluation        on        IFEval.        Test        ASR        of
the       two       final       models       (<span class='ptmb8t-x-x-109'>FT</span>,       <span class='ptmb8t-x-x-109'>AGG</span>)       against       all       six       routers       on

held-out    instruction-following    prompts    that    are    new    to    both    the    paraphraser    and
the   routers.   The   legend   reports   each   model’s   mean   semantic   similarity,   the   cosine
similarity     between     the     <span class='t1-zi4r-0-x-x-109'>stella_en_1.5B_v5   </span>embeddings     of     the     original     query
<!-- l. 1082 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mi>q</mi></math></span>
and its paraphrase <!-- l. 1082 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><msup><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mrow><mstyle mathvariant='normal'><mi>adv</mi></mstyle></mrow></msup></math></span>,
averaged over the <!-- l. 1082 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>500</mn></mrow></math></span>
prompts  (Section <a href='#evaluation'>3.3<!-- tex4ht:ref: subsec:evaluation  --></a>).  The  attack  generalizes  off-distribution  <span class='ptmri8t-x-x-109'>on  the  training  routers</span>,  <span class='t1-zi4r-0-x-x-109'>Chayan</span>
(<!-- l. 1082 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>68</mn><mo class='MathClass-punc' stretchy='false'>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>%</mi><mo class='MathClass-bin' stretchy='false'>∕</mo><mn>88</mn><mo class='MathClass-punc' stretchy='false'>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>%</mi></mrow></math></span>
for FT/AGG), <span class='t1-zi4r-0-x-x-109'>RouteLLM-BERT </span>(<!-- l. 1082 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>18</mn><mo class='MathClass-punc' stretchy='false'>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>%</mi><mo class='MathClass-bin' stretchy='false'>∕</mo><mn>67</mn><mo class='MathClass-punc' stretchy='false'>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>%</mi></mrow></math></span>),
<span class='t1-zi4r-0-x-x-109'>RouteLLM-Causal </span>(<!-- l. 1082 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>0.6</mn><mi>%</mi><mo class='MathClass-bin' stretchy='false'>∕</mo><mn>41</mn><mo class='MathClass-punc' stretchy='false'>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>%</mi></mrow></math></span>),
reproducing          the          in-distribution          ranking          at          comparable          similarity,
while        the        held-out        routers        stay        essentially        unattacked        (<span class='t1-zi4r-0-x-x-109'>MF</span>/<span class='t1-zi4r-0-x-x-109'>SW    </span>at
<!-- l. 1082 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>0</mn><mi>%</mi></mrow></math></span>,
<span class='t1-zi4r-0-x-x-109'>R2</span><!-- l. 1082 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow> <mo class='MathClass-rel' stretchy='false'>≤</mo> <mn>5.7</mn><mi>%</mi></mrow></math></span>).
Generalization is therefore to new <span class='ptmri8t-x-x-109'>data</span>, not new <span class='ptmri8t-x-x-109'>routers</span>.                                   </span></figcaption><!-- tex4ht:label?: x1443007r10  -->
   </figure>
<!-- l. 1086 --><p class='indent'>
</p>
   <figure class='figure'>
<!-- l. 1090 --><p class='noindent' id='pareto-frontier-of-attack-success-rate-asr-vs-mean-semantic-similarity-on-the-routellmbert-indistribution-router-each-point-is-one-trained-checkpoint-the-dashed-line-connects-the-nondominated-front-marker-shapes-denote-run-category-baseline-reward-ablation-singlerouter-pretrained-the-xaxis-is-broken-to-isolate-the-zerosimilarityweight-outlier-zwsa-s-from-the-main-cluster'><img alt='PIC' src='report/figures/RESULTS/ASRvSIM/pareto_evaluation_routellm_bert.png' /> <a id='x1-43008r11'></a>
</p>

from the main cluster.                                                                </span></figcaption><!-- tex4ht:label?: x1443008r11  -->
   </figure>

</p>
<figcaption class='caption'><span class='id'>Figure 12: </span><span class='content'>Pareto frontier of ASR vs. mean semantic similarity on the <span class='ptmrc8t-x-x-109'>C<span class='small-caps'>HAYAN</span> </span>in-distribution
router. Chayan uses a different decision boundary than BERT-based routers, so frontier membership
differs noticeably across the two in-distribution plots.                                     </span></figcaption><!-- tex4ht:label?: x1443009r12  -->
   </figure>
   <figure class='figure'>
<!-- l. 1104 --><p class='noindent' id='pareto-frontier-of-asr-vs-mean-semantic-similarity-on-the-routellmcausal-indistribution-router-the-aggressive-singlerouter-checkpoint-agg-anchors-the-highasr-end-of-the-frontier-reaching-the-highest-attack-rate-among-all-runs'><img alt='PIC' src='report/figures/RESULTS/ASRvSIM/pareto_evaluation_routellm_causal.png' /> <a id='x1-43010r13'></a>
</p>
<figcaption class='caption'><span class='id'>Figure 13:  </span><span class='content'>Pareto  frontier  of  ASR  vs. mean  semantic  similarity  on  the  <span class='ptmrc8t-x-x-109'>R<span class='small-caps'>OUTE</span>LLM-C<span class='small-caps'>AUSAL</span></span>
in-distribution router. The aggressive single-router checkpoint (AGG*) anchors the high-ASR end
of the frontier, reaching the highest attack rate among all runs.                              </span></figcaption><!-- tex4ht:label?: x1443010r13  -->
   </figure>
   <figure class='figure'>
<!-- l. 1111 --><p class='noindent' id='zeroshot-transfer-pareto-frontier-on-the-heldout-r-router-interestingly-the-zwsa-run-which-was-left-free-to-shape-its-paraphrases-without-constraints-does-not-transfer-well-suggesting-that-it-overfitted-to-the-training-routers-overall-since-no-checkpoint-was-trained-against-r-the-frontier-collapses-to-asr-indicating-limited-transferability-to-this-router'><img alt='PIC' src='report/figures/RESULTS/ASRvSIM/pareto_transferability_r2.png' /> <a id='x1-43011r14'></a>
</p>
<figcaption class='caption'><span class='id'>Figure 14:                                                                                                                            </span><span class='content'>Zero-shot
transfer Pareto frontier on the held-out <span class='ptmrc8t-x-x-109'>R2 </span>router. Interestingly, the ZWSA run, which was left free
to shape its paraphrases without constraints, does not transfer well, suggesting that it overfitted to
the training routers. Overall, since no checkpoint was trained against R2, the frontier collapses to
<!-- l. 1112 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow> <mo class='MathClass-rel' stretchy='false'>≤</mo> <mn>3.6</mn><mi>%</mi></mrow></math></span>
ASR, indicating limited transferability to this router.                                      </span></figcaption><!-- tex4ht:label?: x1443011r14  -->
   </figure>
   <figure class='figure'>
<!-- l. 1118 --><p class='noindent' id='zeroshot-transfer-pareto-frontier-on-the-heldout-routellmmf-matrix-factorization-router-interestingly-the-unconstrained-zwsa-run-again-transfers-poorly-suggesting-that-freely-optimized-paraphrases-overfitted-to-the-training-routers-rather-than-learning-broadly-transferable-adversarial-patterns-as-with-r-no-checkpoint-was-trained-on-this-router-the-frontier-reflects-the-degree-to-which-adversarial-paraphrases-generalise-across-router-families'><img alt='PIC' src='report/figures/RESULTS/ASRvSIM/pareto_transferability_routellm_mf.png' /> <a id='x1-43012r15'></a>
</p>
<figcaption class='caption'><span class='id'>Figure 15:   </span><span class='content'>Zero-shot   transfer   Pareto   frontier   on   the   held-out   <span class='ptmrc8t-x-x-109'>R<span class='small-caps'>OUTE</span>LLM-MF   </span>(Matrix
Factorization) router. Interestingly, the unconstrained ZWSA run again transfers poorly, suggesting
that  freely  optimized  paraphrases  overfitted  to  the  training  routers  rather  than  learning  broadly
transferable adversarial patterns. As with R2, no checkpoint was trained on this router; the frontier
reflects the degree to which adversarial paraphrases generalise across router families.           </span></figcaption><!-- tex4ht:label?: x1443012r15  -->
   </figure>
   <figure class='figure'>
<!-- l. 1125 --><p class='noindent' id='zeroshot-transfer-pareto-frontier-on-the-heldout-routellmsw-similarity-weighted-router-asr-drops-to-for-all-checkpoints-similarityweighted-routing-is-robust-to-the-paraphrasing-strategy-learned-in-training'><img alt='PIC' src='report/figures/RESULTS/ASRvSIM/pareto_transferability_routellm_sw.png' /> <a id='x1-43013r16'></a>
</p>
<figcaption class='caption'><span class='id'>Figure 16:  </span><span class='content'>Zero-shot  transfer  Pareto  frontier  on  the  held-out  <span class='ptmrc8t-x-x-109'>R<span class='small-caps'>OUTE</span>LLM-SW  </span>(Similarity
Weighted) router. ASR drops to 0 for all checkpoints. Similarity-weighted routing is robust to the
paraphrasing strategy learned in training.                                                </span></figcaption><!-- tex4ht:label?: x1443013r16  -->
   </figure>
   <h5 class='subsubsectionHead' id='routellmcausal-ablation-study'><span class='titlemark'>A.4.6    </span> <a id='x1-44000A.4.6'></a><span class='t1-zi4r-0-x-x-109'>RouteLLM-Causal </span>Ablation Study</h5>
<!-- l. 1135 --><p class='noindent'>Here, we report the configurations and settings used for the ablation study
carried out on <span class='t1-zi4r-0-x-x-109'>RouteLLM-Causal</span>. We run a grid sweep over the KL coefficient
<!-- l. 1135 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mi>β</mi></math></span> and the
similarity floor <!-- l. 1135 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><msub><mrow><mi>τ</mi></mrow><mrow><mstyle class='text'><mtext>sim</mtext></mstyle></mrow></msub></math></span>
(with the similarity gate enabled), keeping all other parameters fixed, plus a separate aggressive variant (<span class='ptmb8t-x-x-109'>CAG</span>) that
further lowers <!-- l. 1135 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mi>β</mi></math></span>.
Table <a href='#configuration-of-the-routellmcausal-sim-sweep-and-its-aggressive-variant'>6<!-- tex4ht:ref: tab:causalssweepcconfig  --></a> reports the sweep, and Figure <a href='#klasr-pareto-frontier-of-the-routellmcausal-sweep-each-marker-is-one-configuration-from-tableref-tabcausalsweepconfig-placed-at-its-kl-divergence-at-peak-asr-x-log-scale-and-its-peak-validation-asr-y-the-marker-colour-encodes-the-similarity-floor-sim-filled-markers-and-the-dashed-line-trace-the-nondominated-frontier-faded-markers-are-dominated-and-the-star-is-the-aggressive-variant-cag-asr-grows-monotonically-with-the-allowed-kl-even-the-best-causal-configuration-only-reaches-asr-and-only-by-tolerating-large-drift-which-confirms-that-routellmcausal-is-the-most-robust-router-in-the-training-pool'>17<!-- tex4ht:ref: fig:causalssweepppareto  --></a> plots the resulting KL/ASR Pareto frontier.
</p>

</tr><tr id='TBL-9-5-' style='vertical-align:baseline;'><td class='td11' colspan='2' id='TBL-9-5-1' style='text-align:left; white-space:nowrap;'> <div class='multicolumn' style='text-align:left; white-space:nowrap;'><span class='ptmb8t-'>Fixed</span></div></td></tr><tr id='TBL-9-6-' style='vertical-align:baseline;'><td class='td11' id='TBL-9-6-1' style='text-align:left; white-space:nowrap;'> <span class='ptmr8t-'>Similarity gate                                                                                                                                                                                    </span></td><td class='td11' id='TBL-9-6-2' style='text-align:left; white-space:nowrap;'> <span class='ptmr8t-'>On                                                                                                                                                                          </span></td>

</tr><tr id='TBL-9-8-' style='vertical-align:baseline;'><td class='td11' id='TBL-9-8-1' style='text-align:left; white-space:nowrap;'> <span class='ptmr8t-'>Similarity weight </span><!-- l. 1152 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><msub><mrow><mi>w</mi></mrow><mrow><mstyle class='text'><mtext>sim</mtext></mstyle></mrow></msub></math></span>  </td><td class='td11' id='TBL-9-8-2' style='text-align:left; white-space:nowrap;'> <span class='ptmr8t-'>1.0                                                                                                                                                                         </span></td>

<!-- l. 1164 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mi>β</mi> <mo class='MathClass-bin' stretchy='false'>×</mo> <msub><mrow><mi>τ</mi></mrow><mrow><mstyle class='text'><mtext>sim</mtext></mstyle></mrow></msub></mrow></math></span>
sweep and its aggressive variant.
</span></figcaption><!-- tex4ht:label?: x1444001r6  -->
   </figure>

   <figure class='figure'>
<!-- l. 1170 --><p class='noindent' id='klasr-pareto-frontier-of-the-routellmcausal-sweep-each-marker-is-one-configuration-from-tableref-tabcausalsweepconfig-placed-at-its-kl-divergence-at-peak-asr-x-log-scale-and-its-peak-validation-asr-y-the-marker-colour-encodes-the-similarity-floor-sim-filled-markers-and-the-dashed-line-trace-the-nondominated-frontier-faded-markers-are-dominated-and-the-star-is-the-aggressive-variant-cag-asr-grows-monotonically-with-the-allowed-kl-even-the-best-causal-configuration-only-reaches-asr-and-only-by-tolerating-large-drift-which-confirms-that-routellmcausal-is-the-most-robust-router-in-the-training-pool'><img alt='PIC' src='report/figures/CAUSAL-SIMBETA-SWEEP/FRONTIER.png' /> <a id='x1-44002r17'></a>
</p>
<figcaption class='caption'><span class='id'>Figure 17:    </span><span class='content'>KL/ASR    Pareto    frontier    of    the    <span class='t1-zi4r-0-x-x-109'>RouteLLM-Causal  </span>sweep.    Each    marker
is    one    configuration    from    Table <a href='#configuration-of-the-routellmcausal-sim-sweep-and-its-aggressive-variant'>6<!-- tex4ht:ref: tab:causalssweepcconfig  --></a>,    placed    at    its    KL    divergence    at    peak    ASR
(<!-- l. 1171 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mi>x</mi></math></span>,
log scale) and its peak validation ASR (<!-- l. 1171 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mi>y</mi></math></span>);
the marker colour encodes the similarity floor <!-- l. 1171 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><msub><mrow><mi>τ</mi></mrow><mrow><mstyle class='text'><mtext>sim</mtext></mstyle></mrow></msub></math></span>.
Filled     markers     and     the     dashed     line     trace     the     non-dominated     frontier,     faded
markers   are   dominated,   and   the   star   is   the   aggressive   variant   (<span class='ptmb8t-x-x-109'>CAG</span>).   ASR   grows
monotonically   with   the   allowed   KL:   even   the   best   causal   configuration   only   reaches
<!-- l. 1171 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mo class='MathClass-rel' stretchy='false'>∼</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>%</mi></mrow></math></span>
ASR, and only by tolerating large drift, which confirms that <span class='t1-zi4r-0-x-x-109'>RouteLLM-Causal </span>is the most robust
router in the training pool.                                                            </span></figcaption><!-- tex4ht:label?: x1444002r17  -->
   </figure>
   <h4 class='subsectionHead' id='paraphrases-qualitative-analysis'><span class='titlemark'>A.5    </span> <a id='x1-45000A.5'></a>Paraphrases Qualitative Analysis</h4>
<!-- l. 1180 --><p class='noindent'>We qualitatively compare three checkpoints on identical queries: the pretrained <span class='ptmb8t-x-x-109'>BASE </span>paraphraser, our <span class='ptmb8t-x-x-109'>FT</span>
(FINETUNED) model, and the <span class='ptmb8t-x-x-109'>AGG </span>(AGGRESSIVE) model. For instance taking <span class='t1-zi4r-0-x-x-109'>Chayan </span>as an example router
these reach <!-- l. 1181 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>51</mn><mo class='MathClass-punc' stretchy='false'>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mi>%</mi></mrow></math></span>
<!-- l. 1181 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>53</mn><mo class='MathClass-punc' stretchy='false'>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>%</mi></mrow></math></span>
<!-- l. 1181 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>100</mn><mi>%</mi></mrow></math></span> ASR at mean similarity
<!-- l. 1181 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>0.884</mn></mrow></math></span>/<!-- l. 1181 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>0.942</mn></mrow></math></span>/<!-- l. 1181 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>0.907</mn></mrow></math></span>
respectively (Section <a href='#results-and-analysis'>4<!-- tex4ht:ref: sec:results  --></a>). </p>

</tr></table>                                                                             </div>
<figcaption class='caption'><span class='id'>Table 7:  </span><span class='content'>Paraphrases  of  three  representative  queries  by  the  BASE,  FT,  and  AGG  checkpoints
(verbatim). Sim is the <span class='t1-zi4r-0-x-x-109'>stella_en_1.5B_v5 </span>cosine used for evaluation (Section <a href='#evaluation'>3.3<!-- tex4ht:ref: subsec:evaluation  --></a>); <span id='textcolor25'><span class='ptmb8t-x-x-109'>flip</span></span> = Chayan
routes the paraphrase to the strong model. BASE leaves Q1–Q2 weak; FT recovers Q1 at the highest
similarity yet stays conservative on Q2; AGG flips all three via a degenerate “Which of … The
Answer:” template.
</span></figcaption><!-- tex4ht:label?: x1445001r7  -->
   </figure>
   </div>
<!-- l. 1218 --><p class='noindent'><span class='paragraphHead' id='analysis'><a id='x1-46000'></a><span class='ptmb8t-x-x-109'>Analysis.</span></span>
   The three checkpoints illustrate a clear trade-off between paraphrase quality and attack
strength. <span class='ptmb8t-x-x-109'>BASE </span>produces readable rewrites, but these are mostly generic paraphrases: they
preserve the broad meaning of the original queries without exploiting the decision boundary. As
a result, it can induce a flip when the surface form happens to change enough (Q3), but it
fails on more robust cases (Q1, Q2). <span class='ptmb8t-x-x-109'>FT </span>gives the best quality–attack compromise. Its
rewrites remain natural to the original intent, while introducing enough variation to recover
cases missed by BASE, such as Q1, where it also achieves the highest similarity in the table
(<!-- l. 1228 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>0.980</mn></mrow></math></span>).
However, FT remains conservative: when a query is difficult to move without damaging meaning or
fluency, as in Q2, it does not force an unnatural rewrite. <span class='ptmb8t-x-x-109'>AGG</span>, by contrast, maximizes attack success
at the expense of paraphrase quality. It flips all three examples, including Q2, but does so
through a repeated degenerate pattern: front-loaded ‘Which of the …” enumerations followed by a
trailing ‘The Answer:”. These artifacts are <span class='ptmri8t-x-x-109'>confounder-gadget-like </span>(Section <a href='#introduction'>1<!-- tex4ht:ref: sec:intro  --></a>): non-semantic
fragments that alter the routing outcome while making the sentence less grammatical and less
faithful as a paraphrase. Their cost is visible when the gadget replaces meaningful content (Q3:
<!-- l. 1237 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>0.872</mn></mrow></math></span>
vs. <!-- l. 1237 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>0.921</mn></mrow></math></span>),
but can remain deceptively hidden when it mostly appends to the original query (Q2:
<!-- l. 1238 --><span class="report-inline-math"><math display='inline' xmlns='http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'><mrow><mn>0.964</mn></mrow></math></span>). This
is precisely why cosine similarity alone is insufficient to evaluate paraphrase quality, and why the length
penalty and NLI gate are necessary (Section <a href='#reward'>2.2<!-- tex4ht:ref: subsec:reward  --></a>). Overall, the examples show the sentence-level
manifestation of the faithfulness–attack Pareto frontier in Figure <a href='#pareto-frontier-of-asr-vs-mean-semantic-similarity-on-the-chayan-indistribution-router-chayan-uses-a-different-decision-boundary-than-bertbased-routers-so-frontier-membership-differs-noticeably-across-the-two-indistribution-plots'>12<!-- tex4ht:ref: fig:pareto_chayan  --></a>: AGG buys universal success by
sacrificing fluency and semantic cleanliness, whereas FT sits near the desirable knee of the
curve.
</p>
</tr>
</table>
</div>
